But a spiraling black mark had coiled along the rim of his Dark Halo..<br>He realized he could no longer remember the name of his father's church."

The one who taught him to cross his dual pistols on his chest was just an outline.<br>\"You traded his face for the power of this prayer,\" the demon said. \"I'm just collecting the price.\"<br>Vexis was silent for a long time, then stood up to check the magazine.<br>\"One day, I'll pay it off.\"<br>There was no answer—but he felt the presence within him smile. Not in mockery, but with an ancient, almost mournful warmth."

Your mother's death. The hunter's last words. The echo of your first gunshot. That is all.\"<br>He placed his finger on that line of prayer. A faint warmth came from his fingertip, like a letter from a distant past.<br>\"What's your name?\"<br>An even longer silence. Then the voice sounded, without any disguise: \"...Erevan.\"<br>To know a demon's true name is to know how to banish it forever. But he did not speak it. He did not know why."

It was Erevan's.<br>For the first time, he began to wonder: the presence inside him had never truly been a demon."
		"npc_dota_hero_vespera_store_1_title"		"The Ten-Year Mission"

She slipped a letter with no address into a derelict candle-shop in the lower quarters, pushing it through the gap beneath the door. As she turned, she glanced back: the light seeping through the crack was not candlelight—it was a slow, pulsing dark red, as if something vast was breathing inside.<br>The next day, she couldn't help asking the instructor: \"Who was that letter for?\"<br>The instructor did not answer, but she noticed a ring of gray around his left pupil, just like hers.<br>That night, she couldn't sleep for the first time. Not out of fear—but because she suddenly realized: the dark red glow from the doorway and the shadow in her instructor's eyes were the same color."
		"npc_dota_hero_vespera_store_2_title"		"Steps She Caught"
		"npc_dota_hero_vespera_store_2"		"Sometimes, in the labyrinth, there are footsteps. Not monsters. Not other flame-thieves—but steps that follow exactly two paces behind, yet vanish when you turn.<br>She never looks back. An assassin's instinct tells her that the one who owns those steps does not wish to be seen.<br>But once, at the corner of the Divine Remains passage, she quickly turned and caught a glimpse of a back—thin, in black clothes, a dagger hanging at the waist, the same equipment as hers. The back stopped three steps ahead, as if sensing her gaze.<br>Neither moved. She caught a familiar scent: the candle-smoke from the traitor poisoner's attic.<br>\"Who are you?\" she asked.<br>The figure vanished. But the footsteps remained—no longer from behind, but coming from her own direction."

You're dead. But she wipes the dagger exactly like you—three rubs on the knee, then flip it over and rub the other side. You taught me that.\"<br>Pause.<br>\"I didn't talk to her. Beneath it was a dense layer of carvings. Ten. Twenty. Fifty all left by her own blade. Let her continue.\"<br>Her fingers traced the words. She had not written this. Real.<br>They stood inches apart. The dagger's pulse was accelerating now, syncing with the distant heartbeat of Il-Groth at her back. Then it clicked—where the sheath was. Not on her belt. Not on the wall. Nowhere she could ever find. She'd been losing this blade every single cycle. Not dropping it. Letting it go.<br>She closed her hand around the hilt.Every reflection in every mirror closed their eyes."
		"npc_dota_hero_solthra_store_1_title"		"Sizzle"
		"npc_dota_hero_solthra_store_1"		"Sorsha was not named by her parents.<br>At age seven, when the orphanage nuns found her at the doorstep, her swaddling cloth was already half-burned — but she herself was unscathed. Inside the cloth was a note, hastily scrawled: \"Her name is—\"<br>The rest had been burned away.<br>The nuns named her Sorsha—meaning \"Ember.\" They said she must have been abandoned by the Fire God, because no one blessed by flame would burn away their own name.<br>Sparks began to leap from her hair at eight. At first, just small static-like flashes when brushed, then full-blown curtains catching fire whenever she lost her temper. The nuns moved her from the dormitory to the basement, then to the woodshed, until finally she found her place in the kitchen—at least there, fire was welcome.<br>But no one could explain why her hair never sparked while she cooked. Old Mary, the cook, once said: \"The fire isn't cursing her. It's saying what she can't.\"<br>Sorsha didn't understand. But she remembered—because Mary was the last person who looked at her like she was just a child."
		"npc_dota_hero_solthra_store_2_title"		"First Explosion"
		"npc_dota_hero_solthra_store_2"		"At twelve, the clergy came for her. They called her a threat and said she needed to be \"evaluated.\"<br>She hid in the kitchen pantry and barred the door. When the two knights broke through, she screamed.<br>The entire kitchen exploded.<br>No one was killed. But the knights' armor was blown halfway down the hall, the pantry walls turned to dust, and the iron pots melted into a glowing golden liquid—a color iron should never hold.<br>Sorsha stood at the center of the blast, completely unharmed. Around her, the flames still burned—not wood, not cloth, but the air itself, as though reality had been torn open and fire was pouring through the tear.<br>The churchmen did not approach again. One old man in red knelt, voice shaking: \"She is no spellcaster. She is a channel. Fire flows through her.\"<br>She did not understand. She only knew that the kitchen was ruined, and old Mary could no longer cook there.<br>That night, she walked into the church carriage on her own—not to confess, but because she could no longer stand to be near anyone she might burn."
		"npc_dota_hero_solthra_store_3_title"		"Face in the Fire"
		"npc_dota_hero_solthra_store_3"		"On her third cycle into the labyrinth, she saw a face in the fire for the first time.<br>It happened in the moment she burned a sin-crawler to ash — the flames wrapped around the creature, blazing bright orange. But at the heart of that fire, where the heat was most intense, a face appeared. Blurred, twisted, as if seen through a layer of boiling water.<br>A man's face, mouth wide, eyes without pupils, hair shaped like fire.<br>Thorsa froze. The flames died. The face vanished.<br>From then on, she saw it often. Every time she cast, every time she burned, the face would surface at the hottest point of the fire. Sometimes it was screaming; sometimes it was laughing; sometimes its lips were moving — always forming the same words, though she could never make them out.<br>\"What are you saying?\" she shouted into the flames.<br>The fire did not answer. But this time, she caught the shape of the words: two syllables. The first was \"come.\" The second—she couldn't read it before the sin-beast's blood drowned the fire."
		"npc_dota_hero_solthra_store_4_title"		"The Page She Burned"
		"npc_dota_hero_solthra_store_4"		"She once had a diary, about the world beyond the labyrinth. It was only three pages long.<br>The first page was old Mary's stew recipe. The second was about the leafless tree on the orphanage hill. The third was about the first time she saw a boy's face in the fire—not the later man's face, but a real boy who once waved to her from the orphanage gate.<br>She burned it in the fourth cycle by her own hand. Not because she didn't want to keep it, but because she realized that every time she read the third page, she could no longer remember the color of the boy's hair.<br>\"Are you consuming my memories?\" she asked the fire inside her mind.<br>The fire gave no answer.<br>When the diary burned, three faces appeared in the flames: an old woman, a boy, and a young woman she had never met. With grey eyes just like hers. She had no idea who the woman was. After it turned to ash, she knelt by the pile and sifted through the dust with her fingers for a full hour—not looking for Mary's recipe, but for the boy's name. She never found it. The ash was cold. She would write on the walls with charcoal: \"Good things here!\" and \"Don't go this way—I died here once. It wasn't pretty.\""

		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_1_title"		"Exchange"
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_1"		"When the demon offered the bargain, Judicar did not hesitate.<br>\"Take my flesh. Let them leave.\"<br>The demon smiled. Not because it had won, but because it had read countless paladin souls before. Beneath the surface of sacrifice, it always found a quiet desire for glory. It wanted to see Judicar's soul falter, regret, plead at the final moment.<br>It did not.<br>The harvest lasted through the night: bones extracted one by one, muscle stripped in layers, nerves cut like strings. Judicar stayed awake, stayed silent. His lips moved once—not in prayer, but in count: one hundred and thirty-seven. The number of children in the village.<br>The demon stopped. \"What are you counting?\"<br>\"To make sure you don't miss.\"<br>The demon fell silent. It understood now—this knight was not offering himself. He was overseeing.<br>When the last of his flesh was taken, his soul was locked inside the armor. The demon stared at him, and for the first time, it seemed uncertain. \"You have no regret?\"<br>The armor gave no reply. But deep in the helm, in the dark where eyes should have been, a faint, cold glow appeared.<br>The demon stepped back—not in terror. It recognized the color of that light. It was the same color as the knight's own blood, the blood it had swallowed."
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_2_title"		"Silent Cause"
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_2"		"It was not that he could not speak.<br>The remaining nerve endings inside his helm were still capable of moving the jaw, enough to make sound. But he had chosen not to.<br>Because each time he opened his mouth, he heard his voice reflected back from the armor's interior—hollow, metallic, like someone else trapped in an iron shell. It was not his voice. It was the armor speaking.<br>In his first cycle, he spoke twice. Once to a dying thief, who screamed at the sound and then fell still. Once to himself, reciting a prayer to the wall. The echo returned as two words—not what he had said, but a phrase shaped by resonance: Not Worthy.<br>He had not spoken since.<br>But Sorsha was unconvinced. \"You can talk. You just don't want to. That's okay. I'll talk enough for both of us! What you're really thinking is 'Sorsha is so awesome,' right?\"<br>Judicar looked down at her fiery hair. In the dark of his helmet, the two cold points of light flickered—brief, faint, as if someone had let out a quiet laugh from somewhere unreachable."
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_3_title"		"Hammer's Whisper"
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_3"		"His holy hammer was a sword reforged in sin. On its face, four words were carved: No Redemption Through Gate.<br>With every swing, he heard a voice—not from the hammer, but from somewhere deeper. The smallest node of himself, stripped of flesh and memory, still pulsing. A voice light as an old blade being struck against an anvil.<br>It repeated the same words: \"Your weight is far too small.\"<br>At first, he thought it was about the hammer. Later, he understood: it was about him. His soul, scoured of everything, was so light that it left no mark in the world of sin. The monsters he slew—none would remember his hand. The paths he walked—the walls kept no trace of his passing. All his redemption stood before a door with no handle.<br>Still, he swung. Not because the words were false.<br>But because with each fall of the hammer, the words shattered one more time."
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_4_title"		"Aftertone"
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_4"		"At the end of the Divine Remains Labyrinth, before the Mirror of Chaos, he saw the only reflection that truly belonged to him.<br>It was a body of intact flesh. Not the young man he once was, not the paladin he later became—but something earlier. A figure in coarse farmcloth, holding not a blade, but a hoe. The reflection stood in a wheat field, the sun behind him, his face blurred.<br>Judicar raised his holy hammer, and the reflection raised the hoe.<br>Each time he struck, the reflection followed.<br>The hammer and hoe landed simultaneously on opposite sides of the mirror: the wheat field vanished, the reflection shattered, and only a line of text remained on the mirror surface, in his own handwriting, written long, long ago: 'Don't come to save me.'<br>He stared at the words for a long time. He did not remember writing them. Did not remember the field. Did not remember the sun.<br>But he remembered the earth beneath his feet: freshly turned, damp, carrying the scent of grass roots. It was the only smell in his memory that had never been covered by rust or blood.<br>The joints of his armor let out a faint creak. He knelt—not to pray, but to bring his helm closer to those words."
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_5_title"		"Chaos Blossom"
		"npc_dota_hero_seraphon_store_5"		"Sorsha once said the armored one felt nothing.<br>That was not entirely true. Judicar had no physical sense—skin and nerves were gone. He could not tell heat from cold, rough from soft, or pain from relief. But in a rare collision of layers, a sin-beast sent Sorsha flying into him. Her flame nearly out, barely flickering. A single spark from her hair landed on his gauntlet. A tiny point of light, brief, gone.<br>He felt it. Not fire—presence.<br>For an instant, his soul registered a slight warmth, like something had cut through the armor, through the emptiness, through the demon's claim, and touched the last remaining point of him, hidden beneath everything that had been taken.<br>Sorsha stood, brushed off, and her fire came back. \"I'm okay! Don't worry—\"<br>She noticed Judicar had lowered his head. His gauntlet had moved to cover the spot where the spark had died — gently, as if hiding something that might scatter in the wind.<br>\"What are you doing?\"<br>He gave no answer. But the two cold lights in his helm brightened for just a moment—then dimmed.<br>He stood, lifted his hammer, and walked toward the monster. Sorsha followed. And as he left, he still thought about that extinguished warmth and realized it wasn't heat. It was light passing through a gap."
